Friction & strength themes

Open-ended comment themes

Comment sentiment: 22% positive · 31% neutral · 46% negative
We received 626 substantive comments from 265 associates at CA-Woodland Hills (associates could answer up to five open-ended questions). Overall comment sentiment is 46% negative and 22% positive. The top area to address is Focused / Private Work (61% negative across 211 comments), then Physical Environment and Technology. The highest-Friction item is Focused individual work that requires concentration and minimal interruption (Friction 37/100, 64% feel under-supported).

Top priorities to address

Amenities & Services15030%40

What needs attention

  • Desk Availability & Reservations — 174 comments, 52% negative
  • Noise & Distraction — 137 comments, 67% negative
  • Privacy & Confidentiality — 100 comments, 55% negative
  • Hardware (Monitors / Peripherals) — 98 comments, 59% negative
  • Focus / Concentration — 75 comments, 60% negative

What's working

  • Positive / Appreciation — 77 comments, 88% positive
  • Cleanliness & Upkeep — 43 comments, 72% positive
  • Community & Events — 31 comments, 83% positive
  • Reception & Entry — 22 comments, 90% positive
  • Support / Help Services — 19 comments, 73% positive

How comments are categorized: themes are assigned by a transparent keyword taxonomy built for this survey (28 themes across 7 categories), kept separate from the PXIP category set by design so the open-ended read stands on its own. The mapping lives in taxonomy.py and can be tuned without touching the scores.

In their words — what to fix

Often cords or mouses are missing from desks and I do not know who to contact— Director or Manager, More than 10 years · Desk Availability & Reservations · represents ~92 similar comments
Working at a pulse point is challenging due to potential missing items and high noise levels.— Associate, More than 10 years · Noise & Distraction · represents ~93 similar comments
even with designated manager/director space, there is still minimal privacy and sensitive conversations require you to get up and move to a privacy booth which is inconvenient and disruptive.— Director or Manager, More than 10 years · Privacy & Confidentiality · represents ~55 similar comments
Sometimes, find issues of a monitor not working due to settings being changed at times.— Associate, 2 to 5 years · Hardware (Monitors / Peripherals) · represents ~58 similar comments
i know we do not have enough people to start a cafeteria with fresh food with kitchen and cooks. Options in the fridge are very limited.— Associate, More than 10 years · Food & Beverage · represents ~31 similar comments

In their words — what’s working

Carolina is great. Super helpful with anything we ask of her and always has great events. The cleaning staff is amazing. They are always smiling and quick to say hello or Good Moring. I use the gym, lunchroom and coffee areas and they are always clean and stocked with anything needed.— Associate, More than 10 years · Positive / Appreciation
No, the building itself is fine. Could be warmer, but security is kind and friendly, the bathrooms are always clean and the shared areas are clean (like the kitchen/break area).— Associate, More than 10 years · Cleanliness & Upkeep

  • Air Quality and Temperature at PulsePoint Air quality at PulsePoint is extremely poor and is causing frequent respiratory irritation. Even employees without allergies report coughing and sneezing many, including myself and my team, take allergy medication before arriving. Although we’ve confirmed filters are changed regularly, an underlying air quality issue persists—particularly when the HVAC system is running (most notably in winter). Temperature control is also inconsistent across floors. While the first floor is generally comfortable, the second floor is often excessively hot in the mornings, with air conditioning not engaging until the afternoon. In mild morning conditions (e.g., 65–68°F even when the afternoon forecast reaches ~80°F in Woodland Hills), the heating system runs until midday before switching to A/C. This creates an uncomfortable environment, especially for executives in suits. A cooler baseline would be preferable, since layers can be added if needed, whereas overheating (mid-to-high 70s indoors) is difficult to manage. A target range of 69–71°F (up to 73°F) aligns with typical office standards. Also, more small conference rooms and work capsules are needed to support heavy on-site meeting and call demands. Directors are on-site three days per week and are in calls most of the day, which can still be disruptive in open areas. Leaders need capsules (not phone booths) and small rooms for 2+ hours daily for team and cross-functional collaboration. Supporting the Health Plan President is challenging due to limited offices with tables/chairs - only 255-B corner office has this setup - it would be nice to add it to all Staff VP offices. Large CA Health Plan meetings (3/month, 14–25 attendees) often face space constraints more large-room options are needed beyond the single 1st-floor room. — Associate, 2 to 5 years

  • Air Quality and Temperature at PulsePoint Air quality at PulsePoint is extremely poor and is causing frequent respiratory irritation. Even employees without allergies report coughing and sneezing many, including myself and my team, take allergy medication before arriving. Although we’ve confirmed filters are changed regularly, an underlying air quality issue persists—particularly when the HVAC system is running (most notably in winter). Temperature control is also inconsistent across floors. While the first floor is generally comfortable, the second floor is often excessively hot in the mornings, with air conditioning not engaging until the afternoon. In mild morning conditions (e.g., 65–68°F even when the afternoon forecast reaches ~80°F in Woodland Hills), the heating system runs until midday before switching to A/C. This creates an uncomfortable environment, especially for executives in suits. A cooler baseline would be preferable, since layers can be added if needed, whereas overheating (mid-to-high 70s indoors) is difficult to manage. A target range of 69–71°F (up to 73°F) aligns with typical office standards. Also, more small conference rooms and work capsules are needed to support heavy on-site meeting and call demands. Directors are on-site three days per week and are in calls most of the day, which can still be disruptive in open areas. Leaders need capsules (not phone booths) and small rooms for 2+ hours daily for team and cross-functional collaboration. Supporting the Health Plan President is challenging due to limited offices with tables/chairs - only 255-B corner office has this setup - it would be nice to add it to all Staff VP offices. Large CA Health Plan meetings (3/month, 14–25 attendees) often face space constraints more large-room options are needed beyond the single 1st-floor room. — Associate, 2 to 5 years

  • Given that most of our teams are geographically distributed, particularly the product and technology related teams, physical office space often provides limited productivity value. Modern collaboration platforms such as Microsoft Teams, supported by our broader technology stack, already enable efficient communication, coordination, and decision-making across locations. In this environment, requiring colleagues to commute to PulsePoints can create unnecessary friction without materially improving collaboration. When employees travel to an office only to spend the day participating in virtual meetings, the office becomes an added logistical burden rather than a productivity enabler. For many remote and distributed roles, productivity is better supported by flexible work arrangements, intentional collaboration practices, and continued investment in digital tools rather than routine reliance on physical real estate. — Associate, 2 to 5 years

  • Air Quality and Temperature at PulsePoint: Air quality at PulsePoint is extremely poor and is causing frequent respiratory irritation. Even employees without allergies report coughing and sneezing many, including myself and my team, take allergy medication before arriving. Although we’ve confirmed filters are changed regularly, an underlying air quality issue persists—particularly when the HVAC system is running (most notably in winter). Temperature control is also inconsistent across floors. While the first floor is generally comfortable, the second floor is often excessively hot in the mornings, with air conditioning not engaging until the afternoon. In mild morning conditions (e.g., 65–68°F even when the afternoon forecast reaches ~80°F in Woodland Hills), the heating system runs until midday before switching to A/C. This creates an uncomfortable environment, especially for executives in suits. A cooler baseline would be preferable, since layers can be added if needed, whereas overheating (mid-to-high 70s indoors) is difficult to manage. A target range of 69–71°F (up to 73°F) aligns with typical office standards. — Associate, 2 to 5 years

  • Air Quality and Temperature at PulsePoint: Air quality at PulsePoint is extremely poor and is causing frequent respiratory irritation. Even employees without allergies report coughing and sneezing many, including myself and my team, take allergy medication before arriving. Although we’ve confirmed filters are changed regularly, an underlying air quality issue persists—particularly when the HVAC system is running (most notably in winter). Temperature control is also inconsistent across floors. While the first floor is generally comfortable, the second floor is often excessively hot in the mornings, with air conditioning not engaging until the afternoon. In mild morning conditions (e.g., 65–68°F even when the afternoon forecast reaches ~80°F in Woodland Hills), the heating system runs until midday before switching to A/C. This creates an uncomfortable environment, especially for executives in suits. A cooler baseline would be preferable, since layers can be added if needed, whereas overheating (mid-to-high 70s indoors) is difficult to manage. A target range of 69–71°F (up to 73°F) aligns with typical office standards. — Associate, 2 to 5 years

  • Given that most of our teams are geographically distributed, particularly the product and technology related teams, physical office space often provides limited productivity value. Modern collaboration platforms such as Microsoft Teams, supported by our broader technology stack, already enable efficient communication, coordination, and decision-making across locations. In this environment, requiring colleagues to commute to PulsePoints can create unnecessary friction without materially improving collaboration. When employees travel to an office only to spend the day participating in virtual meetings, the office becomes an added logistical burden rather than a productivity enabler. For many remote and distributed roles, productivity is better supported by flexible work arrangements, intentional collaboration practices, and continued investment in digital tools rather than routine reliance on physical real estate. — Associate, 2 to 5 years

In mild morning conditions (e.g., 65–68°F even when the afternoon forecast reaches ~80°F in Woodland Hills), the heating system runs until midday before switching to A/C.   • Given that most of our teams are geographically distributed, particularly the product and technology related teams, physical office space often provides limited productivity value. Modern collaboration platforms such as Microsoft Teams, supported by our broader technology stack, already enable efficient communication, coordination, and decision-making across locations. In this environment, requiring colleagues to commute to PulsePoints can create unnecessary friction without materially improving collaboration. When employees travel to an office only to spend the day participating in virtual meetings, the office becomes an added logistical burden rather than a productivity enabler. For many remote and distributed roles, productivity is better supported by flexible work arrangements, intentional collaboration practices, and continued investment in digital tools rather than routine reliance on physical real estate. — Associate, 2 to 5 years

Data source & method. Built from the PulsePoint survey export · completed responses only · 10 in-scope sites. Support responses map to a 0–100 scale; each dimension is the average of its items; the Overall PulsePoint Experience Index Score is the unweighted average of the four Dimension Scores — the same calculation as the Tableau deck. Friction & RYG per the official Tableau spec · Priority = 40% volume + 35% share negative + 25% Friction.
